# 2026-09-30 — LLM Fleet Visibility, Agent Runtime Creation UI, ACMS Work UX, Required Jira Kickoff, and Business-Idea-to-Product Bootstrap Plan

**Primary agent:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur  
**Date:** 2026-09-30  
**Starting handoff:** `FINAL-HANDOFF-20260929-W4.md`  
**Product example / acceptance fixture:** `home-service-missed-call-agent-ops.md`

**Revision:** Required Jira kickoff and manual/daily reconciliation. This revision supersedes the prior plan's optional-Jira execution paths; all unrelated fleet, runtime, UX, economics, and safety scope remains in force.
**Source plan:** `2026-09-30-llm-fleet-agent-runtime-ui-acms-work-product-bootstrap-plan.md`
**Delivery note:** Revised from the original plan found in Downloads. The desktop authoring environment has no `/mnt/data` sandbox; this copy is delivered from its outputs directory.

## Purpose

Continue from Window 4, but shift the next execution window toward **human-visible product usability**.

The current backend/control-plane work is substantially ahead of the operator UI. The next sprint should make the system usable by a human administrator who wants to:

```text
1. see every VM/runtime that is actually serving or capable of serving an LLM
2. understand what model is running where and whether it is healthy
3. create/instantiate an AI agent from the Server Manager / Agent Runtime Manager UI
4. create/link a high-level Jira task and prepare ACMS work under it
5. create a new Product/Project from a business idea or Markdown brief
6. create the Git repository + baseline requirements/docs
7. put the Jira ticket in To Start (or configured equivalent) and assign startupteamscompany@gmail.com
8. select Check Jira now, or let the daily poll start eligible work through the Executive Agent
9. pause/stop through Jira or ACMS controls and watch progress, PRs, handoffs, cost, and review state
```

The UX should take inspiration from the **information architecture and workflow clarity** shown in the supplied AssistantHub screenshots:

```text
Dashboard
Queue / active work
Tasks / Work
Inbox / Attention
Projects
Usage
project drill-down
task/work detail
PR/review visibility
```

Do **not** copy AssistantHub branding, proprietary text, or visual assets. Reuse only general interaction patterns that make ACMS easier to operate.

# 2. First priority — investigate why LLM Manager shows only one inference VM

The current LLM Manager UI visibly shows only one inference-host card even though the expected architecture includes a fleet of model-serving machines.

Do not assume the UI is wrong and do not assume the other machines are healthy.

Determine the truth.

## 2.1 Build a fleet inventory from authoritative sources

Cross-check:

```text
LLM Manager database
Server Manager / ARM runtime inventory
Proxmox VM inventory
current power/running state
model-route registry
vLLM / inference health endpoints
recent benchmark/deployment records
```

For every discovered model-serving or model-capable runtime, record:

```text
physical node
VMID
VM hostname
runtime ID
IP/FQDN
power state
guest reachable?
inference service reachable?
registered model(s)
model route/profile
GPU inventory / visible GPU count
desired state
actual state
last heartbeat / last model health
current request/load signal if available
```

Do not hard-code a list into the UI.

## 2.2 Explain the one-host discrepancy

Classify each expected-but-not-shown runtime as one of:

```text
serving and missing from UI
registered but powered off
powered on but inference service stopped
healthy but excluded by query/filter
stale registration
not yet registered in LLM Manager
intentionally retired
unknown / needs attention
```

Create a concise finding in the final handoff.

## 2.3 Fix source-of-truth/query defects first

If the UI only shows one host because the backend endpoint returns only one, fix the backend/model registration/query.

Do not paper over backend omissions with a frontend-only static list.

Checkpoint.

# 3. LLM Manager / Server Manager UI — Model Fleet page

Create a human-usable **Model Fleet** view.

This can live inside the existing LLM Manager / Server Manager admin shell.

## 3.1 Navigation

Add clear top-level navigation such as:

```text
Overview
Model Fleet
Models / Routes
Agent Runtimes
API Keys
Benchmarks / Usage
System
```

Do not expose two confusing entry points for the same concept.

## 3.2 Fleet card/table

Show **all known model-serving/model-capable VMs**, not just currently serving ones.

Minimum columns/fields:

```text
Node / VM
Runtime state
Inference health
Model(s)
Route/profile
GPU
Context limit if known
Last seen
Current state / desired state
Attention
```

Recommended operator states:

```text
SERVING
ONLINE_IDLE
STARTING
STOPPED_EXPECTED
UNREACHABLE
MISCONFIGURED
STALE
```

Do not collapse "VM is powered on" and "model server is healthy" into one green dot.

## 3.3 Drill-down

Clicking a VM/runtime should show:

```text
physical node
VM identity
network identity
GPU inventory
registered models
active routes
health probes
recent deployments
recent errors
usage/requests where available
ARM runtime linkage
restart/reconcile controls only where already authorized
```

Keep PDU hard power out of this normal page.

## 3.4 Empty/offline behavior

A powered-off or idle model VM must still remain visible so the human understands fleet capacity.

Do not hide it merely because inference health is unavailable.

## 3.5 Tests

Cover:

```text
multiple hosts render
powered-off host renders honestly
unreachable inference service != VM offline
stale registration visible
model-less but model-capable runtime visible
filters do not silently hide fleet members
```

Deploy and live-verify against the actual MARION-IA-USA fleet.

Checkpoint.

# 4. Agent Runtime UI — make agent instantiation discoverable

The user currently cannot find any UI for creating an AI agent.

The architectural boundary remains:

```text
LLM Manager
  = model/inference lifecycle

Agent Runtime Manager
  = worker runtime / harness / Bridge lifecycle

ACMS
  = durable agent identity / work semantics / orchestration
```

Therefore **agent instantiation belongs in Agent Runtime Manager / Server Manager**, but it should be reachable from the same operator shell.

## 4.1 Add `Agent Runtimes` navigation

Create a page such as:

```text
/server-manager/agents
```

or the existing route pattern used by the app.

Show:

```text
persistent agent identity
runtime ID
VM/container ID
harness
state
assigned host/node
model route/profile
Bridge health
state-sync health
desired/actual runtime state
current ACMS work if any
last seen
```

## 4.2 `+ New Agent` wizard

A human administrator should be able to instantiate a worker without touching raw APIs.

Wizard steps:

### Step 1 — Identity

```text
agent display name
persistent ACMS agent ID (create/reserve automatically)
description / role
internal vs external/customer baseline
```

### Step 2 — Runtime

```text
harness: Hermes first
runtime type: VM today
runtime policy
host placement: automatic preferred, manual optional
```

Do not make Kubernetes or OCI necessary.

### Step 3 — State baseline

For internal workers:

```text
approved internal Hermes profile/state mechanism
```

For external/customer workers:

```text
agentifyme-external-agent-base
```

Never copy internal memories/profiles into external agents.

### Step 4 — Model

Select logical model profile / route where possible:

```text
worker
fast
code
review
frontier
local-required
```

Prefer logical profile over hard-coded model ID when current backend supports it.

### Step 5 — Budget / runtime policy

Optional:

```text
cloud budget
local budget/usage policy
ALWAYS_ON / KEEP_WARM / MANUAL / other supported policy
```

Do not invent unsupported policy semantics. If current backend lacks a field, show it as unavailable rather than faking it.

### Step 6 — Preview / create

Show the exact plan:

```text
ACMS identity
runtime
host/node
network allocation
state source
model route
estimated resources
```

Then call the existing ARM provisioning path.

Use clone-hygiene fail-closed validation already shipped.

## 4.3 Creation result

On success, route to a runtime detail page showing provisioning progress and Bridge/state-sync health.

On failure, show the durable provisioning error and next recommended action.

Do not leave the user with a generic 500 page.

## 4.4 Disposable test

Create one disposable test agent through the UI if safe.

Do not use VM906.

Destroy the disposable test only if ownership is unambiguous and no unique state exists.

Checkpoint.

The current ACMS Work UI shows a `+ New work item` button, but the supplied screenshot of:

```text
/ui/work/new
```

returns:

```json
{"detail":"work item not found"}
```

This is a blocking usability defect.

## 5.1 Root cause

Investigate route ordering/static-vs-dynamic route behavior and the actual intended form route.

Fix the root cause.

Likely classes include:

```text
/new captured by /{work_item_id}
missing page handler
button target stale after route refactor
UI form never completed
```

Do not merely change the button to another broken route.

## 5.2 Regression tests

Add tests that prove:

```text
GET /ui/work/new → 200 HTML
GET /ui/work/<real-id> → 200 HTML
GET /ui/work/<unknown-id> → correct 404
```

and a UI/route test for the button target.

Deploy this fix before the larger Work redesign.

Checkpoint.

# 7. Work intake — Jira is required for normal human-initiated kickoff

Jira is the human-facing planning, assignment, and approval layer. ACMS owns detailed AI planning, decomposition, execution, sessions, runtime control, budgets, artifacts, economics, and reconciliation. Do not duplicate every ACMS task into Jira.

## 7.1 Required kickoff contract

A human initiates normal work by placing a Jira issue in **To Start**, or an explicitly configured equivalent ready state, and assigning it to **startupteamscompany@gmail.com**. Both conditions are required. ACMS imports/correlates eligible work, assigns its Executive Agent, and starts it subject to existing budget, runtime, authorization, and safety checks.

Resolve that email to the verified Jira account ID during setup and persist the account ID. Match assignments by that ID, not display name or email visibility. Validate the account against the existing integration; do not invent an ID or request new credentials when the existing secret-managed credentials work.

Enforce this contract on the server for every dispatch path: UI, API, scheduler, retry, resume, Executive decomposition, and product bootstrap. A disabled button alone is insufficient. ACMS-local dispatch controls cannot bypass Jira eligibility.

An ACMS draft may be saved without a Jira link, but must be visibly non-executable (`Awaiting Jira linkage/approval`). Draft storage is not an alternative kickoff path. Before any AI planning session or implementation execution, link a high-level Jira issue and pass the gate. Deterministic preview/import/scaffolding may happen before kickoff; AI-generated planning work is execution and uses the gate.

## 7.2 New Work form

Keep title, measurable goal, acceptance criteria, Product/Project, Executive Agent, priority, budget/profile, repository/artifact links, and source. Add:

- Link existing Jira issue / Create high-level Jira issue.
- Jira issue key and URL, verified assignee, current status, last checked time, and eligibility reason.
- Save draft; Create/link Jira; Check Jira now.
- Budget/model/runtime preview before a start request.

Remove unconditional Save + dispatch. If a ticket is not eligible, show the exact missing condition and a Jira link. Creating or linking a ticket does not itself authorize execution. The human sets its ready state and dedicated-account assignment; never silently promote a draft to ready.

Creating an issue may use supplied human form values, but must not silently infer readiness. If creation is unavailable, preserve the draft and offer linking an existing issue; execution remains blocked.

## 7.3 ADR-0011 and hierarchy

One Work Item remains one measurable goal/deliverable. Multiple workers, tasks, sessions, and PRs can support it. Only the Executive Agent creates/decomposes execution Work Items; an authorized admin submits intake through the established Executive/admin service, not a worker bypass. Ordinary workers propose future work in handoffs.

One Jira high-level issue may authorize multiple ACMS Work Items within its approved scope. Persist parent Jira authorization on every descendant; pause/stop applies to the whole execution tree. No extra Jira ticket is required for each internal task. New scope beyond that human-approved goal requires a new or explicitly updated Jira authorization.

Checkpoint.

# 8. New Product / New Idea workflow

This is the most important new product workflow.

A user should be able to go from:

```text
idea
→ product/project
→ repository
→ requirements/docs
→ required high-level Jira task + initial measurable Work
→ human sets ready state and AI-account assignment
→ Jira reconciliation → Executive Agent execution
```

without manually stitching together GitHub, Jira, Markdown files, and ACMS.

## 8.1 Entry point

Add a clear action such as:

```text
+ New Product / Idea
```

from:

```text
ACMS Home
Projects
Work
```

## 8.2 Source options

Support:

```text
Blank idea
Paste text
Upload/import Markdown
Business Idea Generator idea
Existing GitHub repo
Jira issue
```

For this sprint, implement at least:

```text
Blank / manual
Imported Markdown
Business Idea Generator repository import
```

## 8.3 Business Idea Generator integration

Source repository:

```text
startupteams/business_idea_generator
```

Discover the repository structure rather than hard-coding assumptions.

Allow the human to browse/select an idea Markdown file.

Use the supplied:

```text
home-service-missed-call-agent-ops.md
```

as an acceptance fixture.

Extract only grounded metadata that actually exists in the file, for example:

```text
title
slug
persona
pain
category
status
score
market
next validation step
one-line summary
business/product concept
assumptions
MVP direction
```

The sample idea is explicitly **draft/unvalidated**, so the workflow must preserve that status rather than presenting it as validated product requirements.

## 8.4 Idea review screen

Before creating anything, show:

```text
Source idea
Product name
slug
problem
persona
current validation state
proposed repository
proposed docs
required Jira task (create or link), current state/assignee, and execution gate
proposed initial Work
Executive Agent
```

The human can edit names/scoping before creation.

# 9. Product bootstrap — create repository + framework docs

When the human confirms the idea/product bootstrap:

## 9.1 Repository

Create a **private** Startup Teams repository using current organization conventions.

Do not invent a new naming standard if one already exists.

Inspect the canonical Startup Teams project-framework/template repo first.

Show repository name before creation.

## 9.2 Baseline docs

Use the currently approved Startup Teams software-project framework.

At minimum create/adapt:

```text
README.md
BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md

ARCHITECTURE.md
FUTURE_WORK.md
INITIAL_IDEAS.md or source-idea artifact
ADRs/
TDRs/ where convention uses them
FIRST_EXECUTION_PLAN.md or equivalent
```

Do not turn speculative statements from a business-idea brief into confirmed requirements.

Classify them explicitly:

```text
validated requirement
human-approved requirement
assumption
research question
future work
```

Copy or reference the selected idea brief with source metadata.

If a manual hop stage dies on a bare\n  name AFTER copying the maintenance conf, the host\n  `deploy/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf` is already switched —\n  `git checkout -- deploy/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf` BEFORE retrying, or the\n  retry double-applies maintenance content.\n- No `pg_dump`/`psql` on the CT host — run inside the postgres container:\n  `docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env exec -T\n  postgres pg_dump -U acms -Fc acms`.\n- No `curl` in the app image — probe with `docker exec acms-acms-app-1\n  python -c \"import urllib.request; ...\"` on 127.0.0.1:8000.\n- nginx allowlists 10.0.10.0/24 + 10.0.20.0/24 — validate via the VM's own IP\n  `https://10.0.20.122` with `curl -k` (self-signed; MARION has NO home.arpa\n  DNS — raw IPs everywhere).\n- `/version` returns `{version, git_sha, git_sha_short, build_time}` — the\n  git_sha identity check catches wrong-image states instantly. Run it after\n  ANY container recreation.\n- LLDAP 10.0.20.101:3890, base dc=example,dc=com; groups acms-admin/16\n  (jordatech), acms-workers/17, acms-observers/18.\n\n## Docker/Compose pitfalls (from the live drill — see references file)\n\n- **Single-file bind mounts pin the inode**: swapping the file on disk is\n  invisible to the container; HUP reload then serves stale config forever.\n  nginx now uses a directory mount (`./reverse-proxy:/etc/nginx/conf.d`) and\n  the maintenance template is named `*.conf.template` so the include glob\n  never loads it. Never re-introduce a single-file conf mount.\n- **`docker compose up -d <svc>` recreates depends_on services too** and can\n  bring the app back with the default image (`acms-app:local`). Pin\n  `ACMS_APP_IMAGE_TAG=<tag>` on EVERY compose up in tooling.\n- **Never command-substitute `compose build`** — its stdout is the build log\n  and pollutes captured variables (`invalid tag`). Send build output to\n  `>&2`; compute the tag, never capture it.\n- **pipefail + `grep -c` false positive**: `grep -c` exits 1 when the count\n  is 0; capture the count into a variable with `|| true`, compare numerically.\n- Recovery from stuck maintenance mode: `up -d --force-recreate\n  reverse-proxy`, then re-pin the app image, then verify via `/version`.\n\n## Bootstrap hop (chicken-and-egg first deploy)\n\nThe first deployment of the release tooling cannot use the tooling. Manual\nhop with identical guarantees: verified backup → stop app → checkout target\nSHA → build SHA-tagged image → migrate → start → validate → write release\nmetadata (mark `mode=manual-bootstrap`). Proven 2026-09-26 (~2 min window).\n\n## Verification hygiene (file-tool corruption lesson)\n\nGround truth for any file change is `git diff` / `py_compile` / `bash -n` —\nnever the tool result echo. Echoes can mislead in BOTH directions (phantom\nerrors and phantom success; this session had mid-write corruption injecting\ngarbage text). For large/critical writes: verify immediately after writing,\nand if corrupted, repair via a python rewrite with assertion pre-checks on\nknown anchor lines, then re-verify with git diff.\n\n## PR-discipline trap (hit TWICE on 2026-09-29 — mandatory habit)\n\n`gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch` checks the LOCAL checkout out onto\n`main` when it deletes the branch. A following `git push -q` (or any commit +\npush) then goes DIRECT to main — Jordan's token has admin rights, so branch\nprotection silently bypasses (remote prints \"Bypassed rule violations\" but the\npush lands). Happened twice in one window (c2d68d7, 8575982; content was\ngreen, the process was violated). **Habit: after every merge-with-delete-branch,\nrun `git branch --show-current` BEFORE any commit; if on main, `git switch -c\n<branch> origin/main` first.** Never `git push` bare from a merge aftermath.\n\n**PERMANENT FIXES now installed on the agent workstation (window-4, plan §D;\nstill keep the habit — the guard is local to this machine):**\n- `~/.config/git/hooks/pre-push` via global `core.hooksPath` — REFUSES\n  pushes to `main|master|production` before the network is touched\n  (behavior-tested in a scratch repo). Emergency override:\n  `ACMS_ALLOW_PROTECTED_PUSH=1 git push …` (audited in shell history).\n  Server-side `gh pr merge` is a GitHub API operation — unaffected.\n- `~/bin/gh-merge <PR#>` — wraps merge-with-delete-branch, then runs the\n  mandatory branch check and auto-detaches to `origin/main` when the merge\n  landed the checkout on main. PATH via `~/.bashrc`.\n- Durable identity fix (least-privilege AI GitHub identity) = staged spike +\n  HUMAN-SETUP file in the flight-work dir; needs Jordan's web-UI action.\n\n## ADR-0012 completion callback (PRs #45/#46, 2026-09-29, live)\n\n- `POST /api/v1/callbacks/execution-completion` — dedicated scoped token\n  `ACMS_CALLBACK_TOKEN` (never the admin token; unset ⇒ 503 fail-closed).\n  CT122 env has CALLBACK_TOKEN (hex) + `ACMS_CALLBACK_BASE_URL=https://10.0.20.122`\n  in /opt/acms/.env; compose passthroughs merged (PR #47, which also added\n  ACMS_JIRA_* + ACMS_GITHUB_ECONOMICS_TOKEN passthroughs).\n- Identity binding: task must belong to the claimed agent (mismatch → 403 +\n  durable EXECUTION_CALLBACK_REJECTED audit; unknown task → 404 + audit).\n  Duplicate/late → idempotent `already_terminal`. Success → no Attention item.\n- **dispatch_service now PRE-creates the execution task before the bridge\n  call**; the task id travels in the instruction (COMPLETION PROTOCOL block,\n  gated on ACMS_CALLBACK_BASE_URL); `external_task_id` = the REAL A2A run id.\n  The dispatch idempotency ledger MOVED to the EXECUTION_DISPATCHED event\n  metadata (dedupe_key → task_id) — code querying `external_task_id ==\n  \"disp-<key>\"` is stale.\n- **Runtime-driven watcher is now the PRIMARY path (window-4, PR #49, prod\n  3679067):** `acms/run_watcher.py` schedules a bounded in-process watcher at\n  dispatch (RunWatchRegistry, idempotent per task_id) that observes the bridge\n  run and delivers the EXACT authenticated callback on terminal state — model\n  compliance NOT required. Retries 0/10/30/60s on transient failure; permanent\n  rejections (403/404/503) stop immediately; watch lifetime bounded (2h\n  default, RUN_WATCH_EXPIRED audit); reconcile remains fallback. Settings:\n  ACMS_CALLBACK_WATCH_ENABLED (default true), ACMS_CALLBACK_SELF_URL (default\n  in-container loopback :8000 — nginx 403s compose-network sources),\n  ACMS_CALLBACK_WATCH_POLL_SECONDS/MAX_SECONDS. **CRITICAL pitfall: the\n  watcher is an asyncio task in the APP process — dispatch MUST go through\n  the API route (`POST /api/v1/dispatch/work/{id}`). Tests:\n  tests/test_run_watcher.py (7). Hermes api-server still has no\n  run-completion webhook — the ACMS-side watcher is what closes that gap.\n- **Window-3 proof pattern (superseded as the completion authority, kept for\n  reconcile-fallback behavior):** dispatch → session auto-open → Hermes run\n  completes (LiteLLM SpendLogs row = cost-of-record) → worker GLM IGNORED the\n  in-instruction completion protocol → the telemetry-scheduler reconcile sweep\n  closed the task from bridge run status + the orphaned OPEN session (both\n  orderings: task-then-session and session-already-orphaned) → zero manual\n  close.\n- Live endpoint probes from the CT HOST work via curl; from INSIDE the app\n  container nginx 403s (container-network source not allowlisted) — same\n  rule as all CT122 API probing.\n\n## Pointers\n\n- `references/live-drill-2026-09-26.md` — bug-by-bug drill breakdown, exact\n  commands, recovery timeline.\n- `references/hermes-harness-live-tests-2026-09.md` — Hermes 0.17.0 live\n  control-mapping results (pause/resume unsupported, steer/interrupt verified),\n  scratch-profile setup recipe, bridge-to-ACMS wiring.\n- `references/hermes-worker-bringup.md` — the full worker-runtime bring-up recipe\n  (VM clone → Hermes venv install via qga → LLM gateway wiring incl. context caps →\n  api-server platform config shape → systemd unit → verification + traps) as\n  executed on acms-worker-001.\n- `references/ip-collision-10-0-20-203-case-2026-09-29.md` — the template-clone\n  impostor-IP case (symptoms, ARP diagnosis, VM108 renumber, generalization).\n- Repo docs: `deploy/README.md` (operator runbook), ADR-0009 + ADR-0010 (both\n  Accepted 2026-09-26/27), `docs/HARNESS_CONTROL_MAPPING.md`, `SPRINT.md`\n  (current slices), handoffs under `docs/handoffs/`.\n\n## Test-suite hygiene (conftest patterns — keep using these)\n\n- **Migration-vs-model drift is invisible to SQLite unit tests** — create_all\n  builds from MODELS, so a migration that creates a differently-named column\n  (0008 created `commit_shas`, model/ingest expected `commit_shas_json`;\n  every economics write 500'd on prod) passes the whole suite.\n  `tests/test_migration_model_parity.py` (window-4, PR #50) upgrades a real\n  PG (pgserver) through the FULL chain and diffs EVERY ORM-mapped table's\n  column set against the migrated schema. **When adding a new models module,\n  add its import to that test's `_collect()`** or the new tables escape the\n  parity check. Run `tests/test_economics_migration.py`-style pinned-revision\n  chain tests for every migration regardless.\n\n- **The shared SQLite unit DB persists rows across tests in one pytest run** —\n  any suite asserting event/row COUNTS must use the `clean_db` fixture\n  (conftest DELETEs all tables, FK-off during wipe). Symptom when missing:\n  count assertions see prior tests' rows (`assert 2 == 1`-style failures that\n  pass under `-x` in isolation but fail in full runs).\n- `get_settings()` is lru_cached — env-var fixtures must\n  `get_settings.cache_clear()` on entry AND exit (see\n  test_work_creation_guard.py pattern).\n- Endpoint tests: sync `TestClient(app)` + async dependency override\n  (`app.dependency_overrides[get_session] = async gen`), NOT\n  httpx.ASGITransport with a sync client (fails:\n  \"ASGITransport object has no attribute handle_request\").\n- Reconciler/scheduler tests monkeypatch `acms.bridge.get_bridge_for_agent`\n  (the attribute resolved at call time), not the scheduler module.\n- asyncio_mode=auto (pyproject) — plain `async def test_` works; `db` fixture\n  pattern: `async for session in get_session(): yield session`.\n\n## Human-credential playbook (learned 2026-09-29)\n\nWhen a plan authorizes an integration but the credential requires web-UI\ncreation (Atlassian API tokens, GitHub fine-grained PATs — NO REST create\npath exists for either):\n1. {"status": "success", "output": "77:@router.get(\"\")\n105:@router.get(\"/{work_item_id}\")\n179:@router.get(\"/new\")\n199:@router.post(\"/new\")\n227:@router.post(\"/{work_item_id}/edit\")\n251:@router.post(\"/{work_item_id}/assign\")\n269:@router.post(\"/assignments/{assignment_id}/close\")\n288:@router.post(\"/{work_item_id}/handoffs\")\n307:@router.post(\"/{work_item_id}/budget\")\n340:@router.post(\"/{work_item_id}/budget/override\")\n======================================================================\n\"\"\"Work Management UI (feature-delivery plan §13; ACMS-REQ-007/008/013/014/023).\n\nRead pages are open to every mapped role (Administrator, Worker, Observer).\nAll mutations are Administrator-only for this slice (plan §13: workers get\nread-permitted work first; privileged actions arrive with the audit backend).\n\nAssignment honesty (plan §13): until A2A delivery exists (slice 4), every\nassignment is shown as RECORDED only — the UI never implies that a database\nassignment reached the agent.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Form, HTTPException, Request, status\nfrom sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n\nfrom ..db import get_session\nfrom ..models import AgentResponse\nfrom ..registry import list_agents\nfrom ..work_models import (\n    AssignmentCreate,\n    HandoffCreate,\n    WorkItemCreate,\n    WorkItemKind,\n    WorkItemStatus,\n    WorkItemUpdate,\n)\nfrom .. import budget_service\nfrom .. import work_service\nfrom .roles import Role\nfrom .session_auth import current_user\n\nrouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/ui/work\", include_in_schema=False)\n\n# Plan §13: recorded ≠ delivered ≠ accepted. A2A delivery is slice 4.\nDELIVERY_STATE = \"Recorded assignment only — delivery to the agent (A2A) is not implemented yet\"\n\n_STATUSES = [s.value for s in WorkItemStatus]\n_KINDS = [k.value for k in WorkItemKind]\n_CLOSE_STATUSES = (\"COMPLETED\", \"RELEASED\", \"SUSPENDED\")\n\n\ndef _require_admin(user) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Mutations are Administrator-only in this slice (plan §13).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        role = Role(user.role)\n    except ValueError:\n        role = None\n    if role is not Role.ADMINISTRATOR:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN, detail=\"Administrator role required\")\n\n\ndef _badge(kind: str) -> str:\n    return f\"badge badge-{kind}\"\n\n\ndef _view_item(record, parent_title: str | None = None) -> dict:\n    return {\n        \"work_item_id\": record.work_item_id,\n        \"work_key\": getattr(record, \"work_key\", None),\n        \"parent_id\": record.parent_id,\n        \"parent_title\": parent_title,\n        \"kind\": record.kind,\n        \"title\": record.title,\n        \"created_by\": record.created_by or \"—\",\n        \"status\": record.status,\n        \"scope_markdown\": record.scope_markdown,\n        \"created_at\": record.created_at,\n        \"updated_at\": record.updated_at,\n    }\n\n\nasync def _parent_map(db: AsyncSession) -> dict[str, str]:\n    items = await work_service.list_work_items(db)\n    return {i.work_item_id: i.title for i in items}\n\n\n@router.get(\"\")\nasync def work_list(\n    request: Request,\n    status_filter: str | None = None,\n", "tool_calls_made": 2, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

{"status": "success", "output": "@router.get(\"/{work_item_id}\")\nasync def work_detail(\n    request: Request,\n    work_item_id: str,\n    user=Depends(current_user),\n    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session),\n):\n    record = await work_service.get_work_item(db, work_item_id)\n    if record is None:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail=\"work item not found\")\n    parents = await _parent_map(db)\n    parent_title = parents.get(record.parent_id) if record.parent_id else None\n\n    assignments = await work_service.list_assignments(db, work_item_id=work_item_id)\n    # Resolve display names for every assignment's agent (not just the active one).\n    agent_ids = {a.agent_id for a in assignments}\n    agents_by_id = {}\n    if agent_ids:\n        agents = await list_agents(db)\n        agents_by_id = {a.agent_id: a for a in agents}\n    active_assignment = next((a for a in assignments if a.status == \"ACTIVE\"), None)\n    assigned_agent = agents_by_id.get(active_assignment.agent_id) if active_assignment else None\n    routines = []\n    if active_assignment is not None:\n        routines = await work_service.list_routines(db, active_assignment.agent_id)\n======================================================================\n32:app.include_router(work_router)\n33:app.include_router(telemetry_router)\n37:app.include_router(server_manager_router)\n41:app.include_router(runtime_router)\n45:app.include_router(memory_router)\n49:app.include_router(budget_router)\n53:app.include_router(attention_router)\n57:app.include_router(dispatch_router)\n61:app.include_router(sse_router)\n65:app.include_router(economics_router)\n", "tool_calls_made": 2, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

### [2026-09-30T02:52:20.739205+00:00] assistant

**§2 COMPLETE** — root cause found, fixed, deployed to prod:

- **Why only one card:** `_physical_host_for_ip()` led with `ip.startswith("10.0.20.16")` which matches **every** guest (.161–.165 + .168) — all 6 hosts collapsed onto `MIAM-00111`, so `_active_hosts()` promoted only VM102. Verified live: prod's `_active_hosts()` returned **1 host** while 5 hosts served `/v1/models → 200`.
- **Second adjacent bug:** VM103 (rollback, shares MIAM-00111 with active VM102) was silently *dropped* — never hidden inventory (§3.4 violation).
- **PR #69** merged (10 new regression tests, suite 138 ✅), staging auto-deployed, **prod VM114 = `439cf8d` Release ACCEPTED** — `/api/status` now returns all 6 hosts.

Now §5 — the `/ui/work/new` bug on ACMS. Root cause already confirmed: FastAPI route registration order (`/{work_item_id}` at line 105 is registered before `/new` at line 179, so "new" is parsed as an ID). Building the fix:

{"status": "success", "output": "def test_create_and_edit_via_ui(monkeypatch, ui_env):\n    cookies = _login_admin(monkeypatch)\n    # Create through the form endpoint.\n    r = client.post(\n        \"/ui/work/new\",\n        cookies=cookies,\n        data={\"kind\": \"project\", \"parent_id\": \"\", \"title\": \"UI Created Project\", \"scope_markdown\": \"# Scope\"},\n        follow_redirects=False,\n    )\n    assert r.status_code == 303\n    detail_url = r.headers[\"location\"]\n    detail = client.get(detail_url, cookies=cookies)\n    assert detail.status_code == 200\n    assert \"UI Created Project\" in detail.text\n    assert \"# Scope\" in detail.text\n    item_id = detail_url.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[1]\n\n    # Edit title + status.\n    r2 = client.post(\n        f\"/ui/work/{item_id}/edit\",\n        cookies=cookies,\n        data={\"title\": \"UI Created Project v2\", \"status_value\": \"active\", \"scope_markdown\": \"# Scope\"},\n        follow_redirects=False,\n    )\n    assert r2.status_code == 303\n    detail2 = client.get(detail_url, cookies=cookies)\n    assert \"UI Created Project v2\" in detail2.text\n\n\ndef test_work_detail_404(monkeypatch, ui_env):\n    cookies = _login_admin(monkeypatch)\n    r = client.get(\"/ui/work/nonexistent-id\", cookies=cookies, follow_redirects=False)\n    assert r.status_code == 404\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- hierarchy + handoffs\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

UI login is disabled\")\n    minutes = lifetime_minutes if lifetime_minutes is not None else settings.session_lifetime_minutes\n    payload = json.dumps(\n        {\"u\": username, \"r\": role, \"exp\": int(time.time()) + minutes * 60},\n        separators=(\",\", \":\"),\n    )\n    payload_b64 = _b64encode(payload.encode())\n    return f\"{payload_b64}.{_sign(settings.session_secret, payload_b64)}\"\n\n\ndef verify_token(token: str | None) -> SessionUser | None:\n    if not token or \".\" not in token or not session_configured():\n        return None\n    payload_b64, sig = token.split(\".\", 1)\n    settings = get_settings()\n    if not hmac.compare_digest(sig, _sign(settings.session_secret, payload_b64)):\n        return None\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(_b64decode(payload_b64))\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        return None\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n        return None\n    try:\n        expires_at = int(payload.get(\"exp\", 0))\n    except (TypeError, ValueError):\n        return None\n    if expires_at < time.time():\n        return None\n    username, role = payload.get(\"u\"), payload.get(\"r\")\n    if not isinstance(username, str) or not username or not isinstance(role, str) or not role:\n        return None\n    return SessionUser(username, role, expires_at)\n\n\ndef current_user(request: Request) -> SessionUser:\n    \"\"\"FastAPI dependency: resolve the session cookie, else redirect to login.\"\"\"\n    user = verify_token(request.cookies.get(COOKIE_NAME))\n    if user is None:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_303_SEE_OTHER,\n            headers={\"Location\": \"/ui/login\"},\n        )\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}

UI/API layers call this service; budget checks are never\n6|  duplicated in several callers.\n7|- Gate BEFORE new cloud execution: budget execution-check verdict decides.\n8|  - below hard limit  → dispatch\n9|  - soft exceeded     → dispatch + retain the warning in the event trail\n10|  - hard exceeded     → no NEW dispatch; durable event + Attention item\n11|                        (EXECUTION_REJECTED); in-flight work untouched\n12|  - hard + override   → dispatch + audited override trail already exists\n13|- Unknown cost stays UNKNOWN — never a fabricated zero (the budget engine\n14|  itself never blocks on unknown cost; the dispatch path preserves the fact).\n15|- Idempotency: a client-supplied idempotency key (or a stable hash of the\n16|  work/assignment pair) maps to ONE execution task; retries return the\n17|  original task instead of dispatching twice.\n18|- Correlation recorded on the task + event: Work ID/key, assignment, bridge\n19|  run id, budget-check verdict (audit trail), external A2A task id.\n20|\"\"\"\n21|from __future__ import annotations\n22|\n23|import hashlib\n24|import json\n25|from typing import Any\n26|\n27|from sqlalchemy import select\n28|from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n29|\n30|from . import budget_service, work_service\n31|from .session_lifecycle import fail_session_for_dispatch, open_session_for_dispatch\n32|from .bridge import BridgeError, get_bridge_for_agent\n33|from .telemetry_models import AgentEventRecord\n34|from .telemetry_service import add_event\n35|from .work_models import ExecutionTaskCreate, ExecutionTaskRecord, WorkItemRecord\n36|\n37|\n38|class DispatchError(Exception):\n39|    \"\"\"Machine-readable dispatch failure (message = stable error code).\"\"\"\n40|\n41|    def __init__(self, code: str, http_status: int = 409, detail: dict | None = None):\n42|        super().__init__(code)\n43|        self.code = code\n44|        self.http_status = http_status\n45|        self.detail = detail or {}\n46|\n47|\n48|def _idempotency_key(work_item_id: str, assignment_id: str | None,\n49|                     client_key: str | None) -> str:\n50|    \"\"\"Stable dedupe key. [Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

Bearer-gated like the rest of the machine API.\n6|\"\"\"\n7|from __future__ import annotations\n8|\n9|from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, Request, status\n10|from pydantic import BaseModel, Field\n11|from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n12|\n13|from .db import get_session\n14|from .dispatch_service import DispatchError, dispatch_work\n15|from .security import require_admin_token\n16|\n17|router = APIRouter(prefix=\"/api/v1/dispatch\", dependencies=[Depends(require_admin_token)])\n18|\n19|\n20|class DispatchRequest(BaseModel):\n21|    instruction: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20000)\n22|    agent_id: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=36)\n23|    idempotency_key: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=200)\n24|    session_id: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=255)\n25|    actor: str | None = Field(default=None, max_length=128)\n26|\n27|\n28|class DispatchResponse(BaseModel):\n29|    dispatched: bool\n30|    reason: str\n31|    work_item_id: str\n32|    task_id: str | None = None\n33|    external_task_id: str | None = None\n34|    budget_check: dict | None = None\n35|\n36|\n37|@router.post(\"/work/{work_item_id}\", response_model=DispatchResponse)\n38|async def dispatch_work_item(work_item_id: str, body: DispatchRequest,\n39|                             request: Request,\n40|                             db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)) -> DispatchResponse:\n41|    \"\"\"Dispatch NEW cloud execution for a Work Item (REQ-059 gated).\n42|\n43|    - 200 with dispatched=false + reason=hard_budget_exceeded → gate held\n44|      (durable EXECUTION_REJECTED event emitted; visible on /attention).\n45|    - 200 with dispatched=false + reason=duplicate → idempotent replay of an\n46|      in-flight dispatch (original task_id returned).\n47|    - 4xx/5xx DispatchError codes: unknown-work-item (404),\n48|      no-active-assignment (409), agent-mismatch (409), no-bridge-target (503),\n49|      bridge-error (502).\n50|    \"\"\"\n51|    actor = body.actor or getattr(request.state, \"actor\", None) or \"api\"\n52|    try:\n53|        result = await dispatch_work(\n54|            db, work_item_id=work_item_id, instruction=body.instruction,\n55|            agent_id=body.agent_id, idempotency_key=body.idempotency_key,\n56|            session_id=body.session_id, actor=actor,\n57|        )\n58|    except DispatchError as e:\n59|        raise HTTPException(status_code=e.http_status,\n60|                            detail={\"code\": e.code, **e.detail}) from None\n61|    return DispatchResponse(work_item_id=work_item_id, **result)\n62|\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.04}

wins).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        runtimes = client.list_runtimes(acms_agent_id=agent_id)\n    except ServerManagerError as e:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=502, detail=f\"Server Manager error: {e}\")\n    if not runtimes:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"no runtime provisioned for this agent\")\n    return runtimes[0]\n\n\nasync def _agent_exists(db: AsyncSession, agent_id: str) -> None:\n    agent = await db.get(AgentRecord, agent_id)\n    if agent is None:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"agent not found\")\n\n\nasync def _event(db: AsyncSession, agent_id: str, event_type: str, summary: str, meta: dict | None = None):\n    await add_event(db, event_type=event_type, actor_source=\"runtime_lifecycle\",\n                    agent_id=agent_id, summary=summary, metadata=meta or {})\n\n\n@router.get(\"/agents/{agent_id}/runtime\", response_model=RuntimeView)\nasync def get_runtime(agent_id: str, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)) -> RuntimeView:\n    await _agent_exists(db, agent_id)\n    client = _client()\n    rt = await _runtime_for_agent(db, agent_id, client)\n    return RuntimeView(\n        agent_id=agent_id, runtime_id=rt.runtime_id,\n        desired_state=rt.raw.get(\"desired_state\"), actual_state=rt.raw.get(\"actual_state\"),\n        recovery_count=rt.raw.get(\"recovery_count\"), last_error=rt.raw.get(\"last_error\"),\n        last_reconcile_at=rt.raw.get(\"last_reconcile_at\"),\n        node=rt.node, vmid=rt.vmid,\n        state_sync_health=rt.raw.get(\"state_sync_health\"),\n        hermes_state_branch=rt.raw.get(\"hermes_state_branch\"),\n        last_state_commit_sha=rt.raw.get(\"last_state_commit_sha\"),\n        provisioning_request_id=rt.raw.get(\"provisioning_request_id\"),\n    )\n\n\nasync def _apply_desired(db: AsyncSession, agent_id: str, client, runtime_id: str,\n                         desired: str, action: str, reason: str) -> RuntimeActionOut:\n    try:\n        rt = client.set_desired_state(runtime_id, desired, reason=reason)\n    except ServerManagerError as e:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=502, detail=f\"Server Manager error: {e}\")\n    # execute desired state now (ARM reconciler §10B)\n    detail: dict = {\"desired_state\": desired}\n    try:\n        rec = client.reconcile(runtime_id)\n        detail[\"reconcile\"] = rec\n    except ServerManagerError as e:\n        detail[\"reconcile_error\"] = str(e)[:300]\n    await _event(db, agent_id, actual_state=rt.raw.get(\"actual_state\"), detail=detail)\n\n\n@router.post(\"/agents/{agent_id}/runtime/start\", response_model=RuntimeActionOut)\nasync def start_runtime(agent_id: str, body: RuntimeAction, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)):\n    await _agent_exists(db, agent_id)\n    client = _client()\n    rt = await _runtime_for_agent(db, agent_id, client)\n    return await _apply_desired(db, agent_id, client, rt.runtime_id, \"DESIRED_RUNNING\", \"START\", body.reason)\n\n\n@router.post(\"/agents/{agent_id}/runtime/stop\", response_model=RuntimeActionOut)\nasync def stop_runtime(agent_id: str, body: RuntimeAction, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)):\n    \"\"\"Stop = graceful VM shutdown; Work assignments are PRESERVED (plan §9).\"\"\"\n    await _agent_exists(db, agent_id)\n    # surface the external runtime dependency if Work remains assigned\n    from .work_models import AssignmentRecord\n\n    active = (await db.execute(\n        select(AssignmentRecord).where(\n            AssignmentRecord.agent_id == agent_id,\n            AssignmentRecord.status == \"ACTIVE\",\n        ).limit(1)\n    )).scalar_one_or_none()\n    client = _client()\n    rt = await _runtime_for_agent(db, agent_id, client)\n    out = await _apply_desired(db, agent_id, client, rt.runtime_id, \"DESIRED_STOPPED\", \"STOP\", body.reason)\n    if active is not None:\n        await _event(db, agent_id, \"RUNTIME_STOPPED_WITH_ACTIVE_WORK\",\n                     summary=f\"Runtime stopped while Work {active.work_item_id} remains assigned — Work state preserved; runtime dependency active\",\n                     meta={\"assignment_id\": active.assignment_id,\n                           \"assignment_key\": active.assignment_key,\n                           \"work_item_id\": active.work_item_id})\n        await db.commit()\n    return out\n\n\n@router.post(\"/agents/{agent_id}/runtime/restart\", response_model=RuntimeActionOut)\nasync def restart_runtime(agent_id: str, body: RuntimeAction, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)):\n    await _agent_exists(db, agent_id)\n    client = _client()\n    rt = await _runtime_for_agent(db, agent_id, client)\n    await _apply_desired(db, agent_id, client, rt.runtime_id, \"DESIRED_STOPPED\", \"RESTART_STOP\", body.reason)\n    return await _apply_desired(db, agent_id, client, rt.runtime_id, \"DESIRED_RUNNING\", \"RESTART_START\", body.reason)\n\n\n@router.post(\"/agents/{agent_id}/runtime/reconcile\", response_model=RuntimeActionOut)\nasync def reconcile_runtime(agent_id: str, body: RuntimeAction, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_session)):\n    await _agent_exists(db, agent_id)\n    client = _client()\n    rt = await _runtime_for_agent(db, agent_id, client)\n    try:\n \"reconcile\": rec})\n    await db.commit()\n    return RuntimeActionOut(agent_id=agent_id, runtime_id=rt.runtime_id, action=\"reconcile\",\n # 2026-09-30 — LLM Fleet Visibility, Agent Runtime Creation UI, ACMS Work UX, Required Jira Kickoff, and Business-Idea-to-Product Bootstrap Plan

**Primary agent:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur  
**Date:** 2026-09-30  
**Starting handoff:** `FINAL-HANDOFF-20260929-W4.md`  
**Product example / acceptance fixture:** `home-service-missed-call-agent-ops.md`

**Revision:** Required Jira kickoff and manual/daily reconciliation. This revision supersedes the prior plan's optional-Jira execution paths; all unrelated fleet, runtime, UX, economics, and safety scope remains in force.
**Source plan:** `2026-09-30-llm-fleet-agent-runtime-ui-acms-work-product-bootstrap-plan.md`
**Delivery note:** Revised from the original plan found in Downloads. The desktop authoring environment has no `/mnt/data` sandbox; this copy is delivered from its outputs directory.

## Purpose

Continue from Window 4, but shift the next execution window toward **human-visible product usability**.

The current backend/control-plane work is substantially ahead of the operator UI. The next sprint should make the system usable by a human administrator who wants to:

```text
1. see every VM/runtime that is actually serving or capable of serving an LLM
2. understand what model is running where and whether it is healthy
3. create/instantiate an AI agent from the Server Manager / Agent Runtime Manager UI
4. create/link a high-level Jira task and prepare ACMS work under it
5. create a new Product/Project from a business idea or Markdown brief
6. create the Git repository + baseline requirements/docs
7. put the Jira ticket in To Start (or configured equivalent) and assign startupteamscompany@gmail.com
8. select Check Jira now, or let the daily poll start eligible work through the Executive Agent
9. pause/stop through Jira or ACMS controls and watch progress, PRs, handoffs, cost, and review state
```

The UX should take inspiration from the **information architecture and workflow clarity** shown in the supplied AssistantHub screenshots:

```text
Dashboard
Queue / active work
Tasks / Work
Inbox / Attention
Projects
Usage
project drill-down
task/work detail
PR/review visibility
```

Do **not** copy AssistantHub branding, proprietary text, or visual assets. Reuse only general interaction patterns that make ACMS easier to operate.

---

Treat `FINAL-HANDOFF-20260929-W4.md` as authoritative unless live state proves it has moved forward.

## ACMS

```text

alembic: 0009_pr_outcome_column_parity
ADR-0011: Accepted + enforced
ADR-0012: Accepted + runtime watcher live
Jira auth: live
GitHub economics read access: live
economics backfill: 45 PRs ingested

```

```text
historical PRs are not yet correlated to ACMS Work/cost because most predate ACMS dispatch
Jira has no currently validated AI-assigned issue suitable for full live transition proof
```

These do not block draft/planning UI development. Normal human-initiated execution requires a verified Jira issue in the configured ready state assigned to the dedicated AI account. If no suitable live issue exists, use a designated test issue or report live proof as NOT RUN; never bypass the gate.

## LLM Manager / Server Manager / ARM

Expected production baseline:

```text
VM114 prod: ae732e2
ORM Slice 5/6 live
ORM_WRITE_CUTOVER=1
clone hygiene live
GPU raw retention: 90 days

```


No new human action is required to begin this plan.

# 2. First priority — investigate why LLM Manager shows only one inference VM

The current LLM Manager UI visibly shows only one inference-host card even though the expected architecture includes a fleet of model-serving machines.

Do not assume the UI is wrong and do not assume the other machines are healthy.

Determine the truth.

## 2.1 Build a fleet inventory from authoritative sources

Cross-check:

```text
LLM Manager database
Server Manager / ARM runtime inventory
Proxmox VM inventory
current power/running state
model-route registry
vLLM / inference health endpoints
recent benchmark/deployment records
```

For every discovered model-serving or model-capable runtime, record:

```text
physical node
VMID
VM hostname
runtime ID
IP/FQDN
power state
guest reachable?
inference service reachable?
registered model(s)
model route/profile
GPU inventory / visible GPU count
desired state
actual state
last heartbeat / last model health
current request/load signal if available
```

Do not hard-code a list into the UI.

## 2.2 Explain the one-host discrepancy

Classify each expected-but-not-shown runtime as one of:

```text
serving and missing from UI
registered but powered off
powered on but inference service stopped
healthy but excluded by query/filter
stale registration
not yet registered in LLM Manager
intentionally retired
unknown / needs attention
```

Create a concise finding in the final handoff.

## 2.3 Fix source-of-truth/query defects first

If the UI only shows one host because the backend endpoint returns only one, fix the backend/model registration/query.

Do not paper over backend omissions with a frontend-only static list.

Checkpoint.

# 3. LLM Manager / Server Manager UI — Model Fleet page

Create a human-usable **Model Fleet** view.

This can live inside the existing LLM Manager / Server Manager admin shell.

## 3.1 Navigation

Add clear top-level navigation such as:

```text
System
```

## 3.2 Fleet card/table

Minimum columns/fields:

```text
Node / VM
Runtime state
Inference health
Model(s)
Route/profile
GPU
Context limit if known

Current state / desired state
Attention
```

Recommended operator states:

```text
SERVING
ONLINE_IDLE
STARTING
STOPPED_EXPECTED
UNREACHABLE
MISCONFIGURED
STALE
```

Do not collapse "VM is powered on" and "model server is healthy" into one green dot.

## 3.3 Drill-down

Clicking a VM/runtime should show:

```text
physical node
VM identity
network identity
GPU inventory
registered models
active routes

recent deployments
```

Keep PDU hard power out of this normal page.

## 3.4 Empty/offline behavior

A powered-off or idle model VM must still remain visible so the human understands fleet capacity.

Do not hide it merely because inference health is unavailable.

## 3.5 Tests

Cover:

```text
multiple hosts render
powered-off host renders honestly
unreachable inference service != VM offline
stale registration visible
model-less but model-capable runtime visible

```

Deploy and live-verify against the actual MARION-IA-USA fleet.

Checkpoint.

The user currently cannot find any UI for creating an AI agent.

The architectural boundary remains:

```text
  = model/inference lifecycle

```

Therefore **agent instantiation belongs in Agent Runtime Manager / Server Manager**, but it should be reachable from the same operator shell.

## 4.1 Add `Agent Runtimes` navigation

or the existing route pattern used by the app.

```text
state
```

## 4.2 `+ New Agent` wizard

A human administrator should be able to instantiate a worker without touching raw APIs.

Wizard steps:

### Step 1 — Identity

```text
agent display name

description / role
internal vs external/customer baseline
```

### Step 2 — Runtime


Do not make Kubernetes or OCI necessary.

### Step 3 — State baseline

For internal workers:

```text
approved internal Hermes profile/state mechanism
```

For external/customer workers:

```text
agentifyme-external-agent-base
```

Never copy internal memories/profiles into external agents.

### Step 4 — Model

Select logical model profile / route where possible:

```text
worker
fast
code
review
frontier
local-required
```

Prefer logical profile over hard-coded model ID when current backend supports it.

### Step 5 — Budget / runtime policy

Optional:

```text
cloud budget
local budget/usage policy
ALWAYS_ON / KEEP_WARM / MANUAL / other supported policy
```

Do not invent unsupported policy semantics. If current backend lacks a field, show it as unavailable rather than faking it.

### Step 6 — Preview / create

Show the exact plan:

```text
ACMS identity
runtime
host/node
network allocation
state source
model route
estimated resources
```

Then call the existing ARM provisioning path.

Use clone-hygiene fail-closed validation already shipped.

## 4.3 Creation result

On success, route to a runtime detail page showing provisioning progress and Bridge/state-sync health.

On failure, show the durable provisioning error and next recommended action.

Do not leave the user with a generic 500 page.

## 4.4 Disposable test

Create one disposable test agent through the UI if safe.

Do not use VM906.

Destroy the disposable test only if ownership is unambiguous and no unique state exists.

Checkpoint.

# 7. Work intake — Jira is required for normal human-initiated kickoff

Jira is the human-facing planning, assignment, and approval layer. ACMS owns detailed AI planning, decomposition, execution, sessions, runtime control, budgets, artifacts, economics, and reconciliation. Do not duplicate every ACMS task into Jira.

## 7.1 Required kickoff contract

A human initiates normal work by placing a Jira issue in **To Start**, or an explicitly configured equivalent ready state, and assigning it to **startupteamscompany@gmail.com**. Both conditions are required. ACMS imports/correlates eligible work, assigns its Executive Agent, and starts it subject to existing budget, runtime, authorization, and safety checks.

Resolve that email to the verified Jira account ID during setup and persist the account ID. Match assignments by that ID, not display name or email visibility. Validate the account against the existing integration; do not invent an ID or request new credentials when the existing secret-managed credentials work.

Enforce this contract on the server for every dispatch path: UI, API, scheduler, retry, resume, Executive decomposition, and product bootstrap. A disabled button alone is insufficient. ACMS-local dispatch controls cannot bypass Jira eligibility.

An ACMS draft may be saved without a Jira link, but must be visibly non-executable (`Awaiting Jira linkage/approval`). Draft storage is not an alternative kickoff path. Before any AI planning session or implementation execution, link a high-level Jira issue and pass the gate. Deterministic preview/import/scaffolding may happen before kickoff; AI-generated planning work is execution and uses the gate.

## 7.2 New Work form

Keep title, measurable goal, acceptance criteria, Product/Project, Executive Agent, priority, budget/profile, repository/artifact links, and source. Add:

- Link existing Jira issue / Create high-level Jira issue.
- Jira issue key and URL, verified assignee, current status, last checked time, and eligibility reason.
- Save draft; Create/link Jira; Check Jira now.
- Budget/model/runtime preview before a start request.

Remove unconditional Save + dispatch. If a ticket is not eligible, show the exact missing condition and a Jira link. Creating or linking a ticket does not itself authorize execution. The human sets its ready state and dedicated-account assignment; never silently promote a draft to ready.

Creating an issue may use supplied human form values, but must not silently infer readiness. If creation is unavailable, preserve the draft and offer linking an existing issue; execution remains blocked.

## 7.3 ADR-0011 and hierarchy

One Work Item remains one measurable goal/deliverable. Multiple workers, tasks, sessions, and PRs can support it. Only the Executive Agent creates/decomposes execution Work Items; an authorized admin submits intake through the established Executive/admin service, not a worker bypass. Ordinary workers propose future work in handoffs.

One Jira high-level issue may authorize multiple ACMS Work Items within its approved scope. Persist parent Jira authorization on every descendant; pause/stop applies to the whole execution tree. No extra Jira ticket is required for each internal task. New scope beyond that human-approved goal requires a new or explicitly updated Jira authorization.

Checkpoint.
